## 2026-09-25 (pass DG): debug the robot

- Mikey re-sent the DF request list word for word with Not committed. Everything on it had been delivered in DF, so nothing was redone; DF is not committed, so this commit message covers DF and DG (DE was committed, per his message before).
- Debug the robot (kind debug): a robot on a five-by-five grid runs a program, and one step is wrong. Arrows (tech 3, How computers think) move one square up, down, left or right; turns (tech 5, Programs that decide) go forward or turn left or right in place, starting from a stated heading. Run plays the program a step at a time and leaves a trail; tapping a step cycles it and sends the robot back to the start; home says which step was the bug and what it needed, and a bump says which step ran into a rock or the edge.
- The puzzles came from a search (a separate script, not the app's runner) that kept only programs that fail as written and that exactly one single-step change brings home without bumping; the rules test checks the same with the app's own runRobot, so two independent runners agree on all sixteen. Eight puzzles a deck, four a round.

Four decks of six passages: ev3 (reading 3 and 4: feelings shown by actions, facts that explain), ev5 (reading 5 and 8: cause and reason, inference, a twist), ev9 (reading 9 and 10: research as evidence, one person's loss inside a town's, an unsupported ad, an unreliable narrator), ev11 (reading 11, 12 and college: evidence versus the conclusion drawn from it, a delayed claim, a conflict of interest, purpose, a counterargument, images in place of a named season).

## 2026-09-25 (pass DQ): the security pass

- Still not committed: this commit message covers DK to DQ, and the changes zip carries all seven passes' files.
- Reviewed: no eval or runtime code building; no network calls anywhere in the app; React draws all user text; the weekly-notes print sheet escapes names and notes before building its markup; the service worker handles only same-origin GET requests. Nothing needed changing there.
- safeJson (logic.mjs) replaces every JSON.parse of stored values and imported files: it drops __proto__, constructor and prototype keys at every depth, so an imported backup cannot reach an object's prototype, and returns null for anything unreadable. Both file imports refuse files over 50 MB unread (BACKUP_MAX_BYTES).
- Educator PIN tries: a full wrong PIN that is erased, or entered with Enter, counts as a try; every fifth rests the box 30 seconds, doubling to at most 15 minutes (pinLockAfterFailure, pinLockLeft), kept in storage under edusphere_v1_pinlock so a reload does not reset it; a right PIN clears it. A counted try does not wipe a half-typed PIN, and the countdown starts fresh with each lock.
- A Content Security Policy meta in the page head (tools/build-site.mjs): own-site scripts, styles, workers and connections only, data and blob images, data fonts, no plugins, no base changes, no forms. Tested on the real page over a local server: account creation, the classroom and the service worker all work with no policy violations. The Claude preview copy drops the line, since that host frames pages with its own rules.
- Not changed, and said plainly in docs/SECURITY.md: the PIN scramble is simple and a PIN is short, so the device is the real boundary; student PINs are stored as typed; backups are unencrypted files. Encrypted backups and a slower salted PIN scramble are listed for later.

## 2026-09-25 (pass DV): the license engine

- Mikey committed DK to DU; this commit message starts fresh at DV.
- License codes for the free pre-K and kindergarten plan: courseIsFree (pre-K 3, pre-K 4, kindergarten: 86 modules), makeLicenseCode and checkLicenseCode in logic.mjs. A code is TWH1.payload.signature: the payload is { id, name, year, until } and the signature is ECDSA P-256 over it, checked in the app with a public key through the browser's own cryptography, offline. An edited code, an expired code, a code from any other key or plain text is refused with a sentence a parent can act on. One code per child per school year.
- Keys: the app carries only a public key. This one is a development key whose private half sits in tests/fixtures for the tests; before selling, Mikey makes the real pair on his own computer with tools/license-keys.mjs, keeps the private key out of the repository, and only the new public key replaces this one (docs/RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md). tools/license-sign.mjs signs a code for each order.
- Nothing in the app is locked yet: the next pass wires codes into the educator side (enter a code, attach it to a student) and limits grade 1 and up to licensed students, with the walkthrough still showing everything.
- docs/TEFA-PLAN.md gained notes on depth (30 to 45 skills per core subject per grade, grades 1 to 5 math and reading first), accreditation (accreditors accredit institutions, not curriculum alone; Texas IMRA review as supplemental materials is the realistic quality stamp), and running the site at thewisehuman.com under an assumed name for iECHO, LLC.

## 2026-09-26 (pass EF): desktop dragging, an empty Notes section, centering, quick checks wording

- Mikey committed DV to EE; this commit message starts fresh at EF.
- Dragging on a desktop (Mikey: nothing dragged, though a long press looked as if it would). In Chromium a mouse drag already worked, so the fault was in what other browsers do with a mouse press: it can start the browser's own selection or picture drag, which cancels the pointer before a game sees it move, and Safari can refuse pointer capture, which threw inside each game's start and stopped the drag before it began. Every drag start now goes through grabPointer: it prevents the default, asks for capture inside try/catch (moves over the board still bubble to it), and the board adds WebkitUserSelect and WebkitTouchCallout none; a document listener stops dragstart inside any game board. Nine drag starts use it (seven games, the two mazes and the drawing pad).
- tests/e2e/drag.mjs, a new check.sh step: a desktop mouse drags a piece, selection is off, the browser's own dragstart is prevented inside a board, and a drag still works when setPointerCapture throws. Against the old code the last two failed, which is the proof the fix addresses the fault.
- The report's Notes card takes edu-no-print when the student has no notes, so it stays on screen for writing one and leaves the printed report. My Completed Skills and its hide checkbox are centered. The quick checks paragraph uses Mikey's words with a line break before the second sentence.

## 2026-09-26 (pass EG): dragging student cards on the Classroom page

- Mikey: the drag he meant was reordering students on My Classroom, not the games. EF is treated as not yet committed, so this commit message covers EF and EG, and the changes zip carries both passes' files.
- The fault: each card listened for its own pointer moves, and only the held card acted on them. A finger is captured by the card it first touched, so phones worked; a mouse is not, so once the pointer left the held card its moves went to other cards and were ignored. The card lifted after the long press and then nothing happened.
- The fix: while a card is held, a window-level listener follows the pointer (mouse, pen or finger), the held card rides under it (translateY that counts page scroll), and the drop spot is worked out among the other cards only, by which card's middle the pointer is above, with the green line on that card's top edge or under the last card for the end. Letting go anywhere drops; a second end is ignored. Text selection and the browser's own dragstart are stopped while a card is held, a right click does not start a hold, and holding near the top or bottom 70 pixels of the screen scrolls the page. The drop now always lands where the line shows (before, dragging downward placed the card one row later than its line).
- tests/e2e/drag.mjs gained three checks: a held card follows a desktop mouse, letting go below the others moves the student to the end, and the new order is saved in the roster. Against the old code all three failed. A one-off touch run confirmed a finger drag still follows and drops where the line shows.

## 2026-09-28 (pass EW): Wonder questions to Mikey's rules; why one game is open

- EV was not committed, so this commit message covers EV and EW, and the changes zip carries both passes' files.
- Mikey restated the Wonder rules: one per every two modules; reframing-failure and handling-tough-emotion questions prioritized when modules are failed or several questions are missed in a row; never the same question more than twice; pools large enough for years of failures.
- The audit found three faults. The cadence counted rounds, not modules (the log of 2026-09-11 says every two mastered modules), so a module passed twice for its star counted double; nextWonder now counts distinct modules worked since the last reflection, pass or fail. The failure-first rule could never fire: the result screen offered a Wonder only after a passed round, so the last round was never a failure; the screen now offers a due Wonder after any finished round. And the comfort preference sorted after times-answered, so an unseen world question beat a comfort question seen once; comfort now sorts first.
- A rough patch (MISS_STREAK = 3, missStreak reads the round's core answers in order) is a failed round or three misses in a row inside any round since the last reflection. After one, failure and feelings questions come first, then hard-days questions, then the rest; within each, the least-answered and the theme met longest ago.
- Supply standard, now a rule test: at one reflection per two modules, each stage's pool shown twice covers its reflections half again, and its failure and feelings questions shown twice cover three reflections in four (pre-readers' spoken questions too). Before: grades 9 to college held 28 questions for 62 reflections and only 9 on failure or feelings. 43 new questions (20 grown, 12 teen, 9 growing, 2 early with spoken versions), 37 of them on failure or feelings, bring the pool to 190: early 56 (27 failure or feelings), growing 50 (22), teen 36 (18), grown 48 (25). Scientist lines were checked and hedged where research is mixed (the spotlight effect as about double, social pain as studies suggest, reappraising nerves as excitement, putting feelings into words). New questions await the educator's review like all others.
- The browser test now expects no reflection after redoing module one and one right after the failed round in module two. Mikey also asked why a new student sees one game: by design (2026-09-21 and the course-games decision), Star is open from the first day with the first game on the student's own list, and the rest unlock as courses are finished.

## 2026-09-28 (pass FR): every story on the Story Log and in the book, held by a rule test

- Mikey committed through FQ; this commit message starts fresh at FR. First pass of the depth program: the guard rail before any new course is built.
- The Story Log's lists and the story book's pages now come from logic.mjs, so a rule test can walk them the way the screens do: `storyLogAvailable(events, storyFor, courseStoryFor)` names every module story on a student's courses and every long story, each with its first-read time and whether it is unlocked; `recentReads(events, now)` is Most recent; `bookPages(chapters, courseStory, hasArt)` turns a course into printed sheets; `printParts` now reports the inches each part uses. The screens only sort and word what logic gives them.
- The rule test (tests/stories.test.mjs): a fresh student's Story Log lists all 454 module stories unread and all 62 long stories locked with every module to go; reading a story moves it to Read at its first opening and into Most recent; every long story belongs to a real course; every title shows. Then every one of the 81 courses is paged twice, with nothing painted and with everything painted: each story appears once in course order, every paragraph lands on exactly one sheet with no gap, every picture is on the sheet of its own paragraph, no sheet is fuller than the paper (8.95 inches), and every paragraph fits a sheet of its own beside its picture under a title and main painting, so the book never has to part the two. Painted, the books come to 1,321 sheets. The click-through still prints real PDFs of two books; this test covers the other seventy-nine.
- Caught and fixed: Most recent showed the first three stories a student ever opened, not the last three. `storiesRead` lists first openings in the order they happened, and the screen took the top of that list. Now it is the last three, newest first, and the Read list is newest first too.
- Caught and fixed: the What's new pop-up had been dark since September 24. tools/build-site.mjs read the heading as one word (the pass BQ fix assumed `## 2026-09-22`), so when the headings became `## September 24, 2026 (a helping hand)` nothing matched and every build since baked `__eduNews = null` into the page; no teacher saw a note after that day. tools/whats-new.mjs now picks the block with the latest date (the higher one on a tie) and the stamp is the whole heading, so two blocks on one day both show in turn. docs/WHATS-NEW.md is ordered newest first again (the two September 24 blocks that sat above the September 28 ones moved down to their day), and tests/site.test.mjs opens the real page and checks the pop-up shows that block whole, then stays closed after a reload.
- Caught and fixed: on a phone the pop-up waited for the first week tour, which only runs on a wide screen, so a phone-only educator never saw it. The effect at the top of the app now opens the news on a phone straight away and still waits for the tour on a wide screen.
- Next: pass FS, computer science K to 2, the first course of the depth program, built complete in one pass.

Each has a spoken lesson with three ways of saying it, six spoken questions with tapped answers, every answer said in the lesson first (tools/untaught.mjs reports zero), and a quick check like every module.
- Standards: the Texas codes come from TEA's own breakouts document for §126.1 (Kindergarten, adopted 2022, in force from 2024-2025): (b)(8)(B) input and output hardware, (1)(A) decompose a task, (1)(C) algorithms with first, next, then, last, (1)(B) patterns and predictions, (2)(A) a sequence of code, (7)(A) keep an account safe and log off, (7)(B) what is safe to share, (5)(A) responsible behavior; the national codes are CSTA level 1A (1A-CS-02, 1A-AP-08, 1A-AP-11, 1A-AP-10, 1A-IC-18, 1A-NI-04, 1A-IC-17). The plan row is marked ready and coverage passes.
- Fixed on the way: TEA's table of contents numbers the technology sections §126.1 K, §126.2 grade 1, §126.3 grade 2, §126.8 grade 3, §126.9 grade 4, §126.10 grade 5, §126.17 grade 6, §126.18 grade 7, §126.19 grade 8. The older technology courses cited §126.7 for grade 3, §126.9 for grade 5 and §126.15 for grade 7, all wrong, now §126.8, §126.10 and §126.18 in the plan and in every module source. Their sub-codes ((b)(4)(A) and the like) were not re-read against the breakouts and stay on the open items list.

Read-aloud, flow, arc, spread and art audits all clean; docs/STORY-LENGTH.md still reads 0 short. Four lesson coloring pages D163 to D166. The ledger holds 3,154 pictures marked Needed; docs/LEONARDO-PROMPTS.csv regenerated.
- The game: Walk the Robot, a new kind, the app's twenty-first, (`walk`), owned by the course and unlocked by finishing it. No program is given: the child taps arrows to give the robot its steps, taps a step to take it back, taps Go and watches the robot follow them to the star; a bump into a rock or the edge stops the run and keeps the steps for fixing. Eight walks in ROBOT_DECKS.walk, each reachable in five steps or fewer (a rules test finds the path). The click-through plays two robots, one home first time and one after a bump. ## 2026-09-29 (pass FT): the plain AI course, for the 6 to 8 band

- Mikey has not committed FS, so the commit message covers FS and FT. He left the band to Claude's judgment of a child's development: grades 6 to 8, at grade 6 (the youngest grade of the band), because a child of eleven or twelve can hold the ideas the course turns on and is already meeting the tools.
- The course: `tech-6`, What a machine learns, four modules in order: learning-from-examples (a program shown thousands of labeled examples keeps the patterns they share; the result is a model, a generalization, and a language model builds an answer one likely word at a time), wrong-and-sure (a model's number is fit, not truth; a made-up date that looks right is a hallucination; sounding sure is a pattern too; a second source, and the question how do you know?), fair-examples (a model knows only its examples; the unfairness was in the pile before the model ran; look at who is missing and test on every group), staying-in-charge (the text box is a postcard; a digital footprint; the work inside is someone's intellectual property, and copyright says who may copy it; use the tool to learn, not to skip learning). Six questions a module, every answer said in its lesson first; the untaught report reads zero.
- Standards from TEA's breakouts for §126.17 (grade 6, adopted 2022): (1)(B) patterns in representations of data, (1)(C) abstraction and generalized against specific information, (6)(A) inferences from data, (9)(D) information exaggerated or misrepresented online, (9)(A) safe, ethical and positive online behavior, (8)(A) the digital footprint, (9)(B) intellectual property including copyright, (4)(C) transferring knowledge to new technologies; CSTA level 2: 2-DA-09, 2-DA-08, 2-IC-21, 2-IC-23, 2-IC-20. Coverage passes with the row marked ready.
- Stories at the older length with six pictures each (S2722 to S2745): The Bird Feeder That Learned, The Bridge That Was Never Built, The Voice It Never Heard, The Postcard Rule; and the long story Frederick and the Jar of Pond Water (CS379 to CS384, 447 words, eight paragraphs, Frederick at twenty in the lab, Character: C5), the young-adult chapter of his storyline: the model counted the pond creatures fast and was wrong about the one it had seen least. Art, length, arc and flow audits clean (the flow count stays at 135). The ledger holds 3,184 pictures marked Needed.
- The game: Teach the Robot, a new kind, the app's twenty-second, (`teach`), owned by the course. Twelve fruits on a map; the robot knows two and guesses the rest from its nearest known fruit, shown as a red or yellow ring; the child taps a wrong guess to teach it and every guess is made again from all it now knows. Four boards made by a small search so each starts with two or more wrong guesses and is right after five lessons or fewer; the rules test walks every board with the same guessing function the screen uses, and the click-through teaches a board to done. The score is lessons, lower is better: the child learns that the fix for a wrong guess is the missing example, not a faster machine.
- Wonder: two questions for the teen stage tied to the course, one a failure question (a sure and wrong answer you repeated: whose mistake?) and one about why learn to write when a machine can.
- Next: computer science 9 to 12, the band with no course yet, then economics and personal finance by band, with a college course every few passes.

## 2026-09-29 (pass FU): computer science for the 9 to 12 band

A timer set inside the tap, not in an effect keyed on the sum, because such an effect clears its own timer the moment the lit state changes; the click-through makes 79 and sees the next target arrive.
- Wonder: two questions for the grown stage tied to the course, a failure question (a program does exactly what you wrote, not what you meant: flaw or mirror?) and one on a photograph being numbers.

Where there is arithmetic the answer is computed fresh: an element's address, values a byte or two bytes hold, looks for a binary search, comparisons among n items, what a stack or queue gives back, a hash slot, a factorial, a folder count.

## 2026-09-29 (pass GI): philosophy for the 9 to 12 band, the fourth strand begins

- Mikey committed through GH; this commit message starts fresh at GI. Philosophy is a new subject, the eleventh, ranked after Economics; the sort of subjects falls back to 99 for anything unranked so a new subject can never land among the old ones by accident.
- The band: 9 to 12 first, because the strand's core (validity and soundness, the fallacies, epistemology, ethics) sits most naturally with students who can already read an argument; the younger bands come next with philosophy for children in mind.
- The course: `philosophy-9`, How to think about anything, grade 9. Four modules: what-is-an-argument (premises and conclusion; valid against sound; modus ponens and modus tollens; deduction against induction; the hidden premise), the-usual-mistakes (nine fallacies by name, the principle of charity, the burden of proof), what-can-we-know (justified true belief and Gettier, 1963; perception, memory, testimony and reason; Descartes, 1641, and Hume, 1748; Popper's falsifiability in the 1930s; Ockham's razor stated as "do not add assumptions you do not need"), how-should-we-live (Mill's utilitarianism, 1861; Kant's test, 1785; Aristotle's virtues and the mean; Philippa Foot's trolley, 1967; Rawls's veil of ignorance, 1971). Every date is one I know to be right.
- Standards: Texas has no philosophy TEKS, so the Texas column carries the College and Career Readiness Standards, Cross-Disciplinary Standards, Key Cognitive Skills: I.A.1, I.A.2, I.B.1 to I.B.4, I.F.2, I.F.3 and I.F.4; the national column carries the Common Core anchor standards for argument, RI.9-10.8, SL.9-10.3 and W.9-10.1, quoted as published. The plan row is marked ready and coverage passes.
- Stories at the older length with six pictures each (S2978 to S3001): The Penguin in the Syllogism, The Third Door, The Horoscope and the Forecast, The Switch and the Cake; and the long story Frederick and the Burden of Proof (CS445 to CS450, 458 words, eight paragraphs, six pictures, Frederick at twenty, Character: C5): a copper bracelet, a napkin with two lines on it, and the question of whether to say so at all, in which the trials he cites (copper bracelets against plain ones, no measurable difference) are real. Four sentences over 32 words and two choppy paragraphs were caught by the tests and audits and fixed; the flow count stays at 135. The ledger holds 3,514 pictures marked Needed.
- The game: Valid or Not, a new kind (`valid`, the app's thirty-third), owned by the course: an argument in three lines built from everyday sentences in one of four forms, and one question, does the form guarantee the conclusion; the right answer names the form, a wrong one wobbles; six arguments a round and the clock counts up. `ARGUMENT_PAIRS`, `ARGUMENT_FORMS` and `argumentRounds(round)` in logic.mjs are the one place the arguments come from; the rules test proves validity follows the form and that the two valid forms are exactly modus ponens and modus tollens, and the click-through sorts six by the answer the board carries in `data-argument-valid`.
- Wonder: two grown-stage questions tied to the course, one about voicing a single doubt in a room that agrees and one a failure question about winning an argument the wrong way.
- Next: philosophy for the younger bands (6 to 8 as `philosophy-6`: questions, reasons and disagreement for that age; then 3 to 5 and K to 2 in the philosophy-for-children tradition), then psychology (§113.45 Psychology, Social Studies, read from TEA's text before citing), speech, agriculture and business, with a college course every few passes.

Four modules: how-psychologists-know (what psychology studies and how it differs from sociology and biology; Wundt's laboratory of 1879; the six perspectives; principle against theory; observation, survey, correlation and experiment, and why only an experiment shows cause; APA ethics; mean, median, mode, range and standard deviation, with the statistics computed fresh in the questions), brain-senses-and-growing-up (central and peripheral nervous systems, neurons, adrenaline and cortisol; sensation against perception, thresholds, adaptation, illusions; nature and nurture; Piaget's four stages and Erikson's eight), learning-memory-and-thinking (Pavlov, Skinner and Bandura's 1961 doll; encoding, storage and retrieval, Miller's seven of 1956, Ebbinghaus's curve of 1885 and the spaced review this app runs on; algorithms and heuristics, functional fixedness and confirmation bias; Maslow's 1943 pyramid), people-among-people (the fundamental attribution error; bias and discrimination; Asch 1951, Milgram 1961, Darley and Latané 1968 and the one-name cure for the bystander effect; altruism; attitudes; the stress response and the coping strategies that hold up under study; the stigma of labels). Every study named carries its year and each is one the course can defend.
- Standards: §113.45 Psychology (One-Half Credit), Beginning with School Year 2011-2012, read from the published text this turn; TEA's Chapter 113 listing still carries that version. Twenty-nine student expectations are cited across (c)(1) to (c)(13). The national column carries the APA National Standards for High School Psychology Curricula by pillar. The plan row is marked ready and coverage passes. Nine prompts ran past the 70-character limit the question rule keeps and were shortened.

## 2026-09-30 (pass GV): five fixes from Mikey's laptop screenshot

- Mikey committed GU, so the commit message starts fresh at GV. This pass answers his list; the curriculum resumes next pass.
- Search boxes clear in one tap: the courses search, the notes search and the Find-your-name box now show a small, quiet × at the right edge, inside the box, whenever there is text to clear (`ClearableInput` in ui.jsx, aria-label "Clear search"). The browser test types a search, sees the ×, taps it and finds the box empty and the × gone.
- A refresh keeps the page: when a real student (not a walk-through) is on their overview, in a lesson, on a story or on the map, the app writes a small note to the browser (`edusphere_v1_where`: the student, the screen and the module) and a reload loads that student and returns them to that screen, with the module for a lesson or story. Educator screens are never remembered, because they sit behind the PIN, and the note is erased when the student taps Exit. The test page's fake storage now keeps its keys in localStorage the way the real index.html shim does, so the browser test can reload mid-lesson and prove the student lands back in the same lesson; the one check that read the fake store directly reads localStorage now.
- Elective grade text shows the band: a band elective sits at the youngest grade of its band but serves the whole band, so "Grade 3" beside it made the grade list look full of gaps. `courseGradeLabel(course)` in logic.mjs returns the course's `audience` for an elective ("Grades 3 to 5", "Kindergarten to grade 2", "Grades 9 to 12", "College level") and the grade for everything else; the story book dropdown and heading, the course preview line and `courseLabel()` (the text in parentheses on every course row) use it.
- Weekly note title: on screens 700 pixels and wider the header is a three-column grid, so the title is centered over the whole bar and Edit and Print stay at the right; on a phone the bar is unchanged.
- Back to recommended courses: Mikey asked what it does, and the code says: it returns the list to our recommendation plus every course with any progress plus what those unlock, and clears the elective picks, so any assigned course that has not been started yet, including an elective, is removed, and a course with any progress stays. An info button beside the link now says exactly that, with the link's name in italics.
- Every block added this pass opens with a plain-English comment.
- Next: agriculture for 6 to 8 as `agriculture-6`, then 3 to 5 and K to 2, then business from Chapter 130 Subchapter D, with a college course every few passes.

## 2026-09-30 (pass HB): the starter PIN retired, and agriculture for 3 to 5

An earlier attempt at this pass was cut off after building almost all of it and before any docs; this turn read every piece of it, checked it against its sources, corrected what was wrong, and finished it.

With no account, Educator Login opens the account setup with the device name and state filled in, and the PIN screen shows no PIN box, only a way to create the account. Start over as a new educator now also ends the educator session. The one thing no code can close: on a device with no account, whoever creates the account first chooses its PIN, so the account should be created as soon as a device holds students. The educator test now proves the no-account routes and the PIN screen.
- The course: `agriculture-3`, Down on the farm, grade 3 under Agriculture, for grades 3 to 5. Four modules: farms-and-food (food and fiber start on farms; Texas first in cattle and cotton; the natural resources farms use; the path of a loaf of bread and the jobs along it), seeds-and-soil-3 (soil from weathered rock and decomposition; most seeds, like beans and radishes, sprouting with water, air and warmth before they need light; the lima bean life cycle; radishes in about a month; a pecan tree's winter dormancy), farm-animals-3 (food, water, shelter and space; flat teeth and a four-part stomach, webbed feet, wool; a chick in about 21 days and a laying hen at about five months; monarchs migrating through Texas), a-tiny-farm (a plan, a tally, a bar graph and measurements as evidence; the food chain from the sun and the bees a squash needs; drought and flood; windbreaks, crop residue and reduce, reuse, recycle as stewardship).

The same kind of abridgement was found in the grade 6 agriculture row written in pass GX, §112.26(b)(1)(D), and restored to its full wording too.
- Facts checked line by line in the lessons, the four stories and the long story. Two corrections: a story said a cow can drink "up to twenty gallons on a hot day", which made twenty a ceiling, and now says twenty gallons or more; and the game's seed is now a bean, because its rule, that light never decides whether a seed sprouts, is true for beans and radishes but not for every seed (lettuce needs light to start).
- Stories at the older length with six pictures each (S3310 to S3333): The Path of a Loaf of Bread, The Cup in the Closet, Four Questions Every Morning, The Squash That Would Not Come; and the long story Mike and the Twenty-One Days (CS529 to CS534, 424 words, eight paragraphs, six pictures, Mike bringing an incubator and twelve eggs to a third-grade class, Character: C2): eggs turned by hand, candled on day seven, left alone from day eighteen, and hatched on day twenty-one. The flow count stays at 135. The ledger holds 3,942 pictures marked Needed.
- The game: Will It Sprout?, a new kind (`sprout`, the app's forty-seventh), owned by the course: a bean seed in a cup with four facts, water, air, warmth and light, and two answers; `sproutAnswer` holds the one rule and `sproutRounds(round)` builds six seeds with both answers and at least one bean that sprouts in the dark, which the rules test proves; the click-through plays a round.
- Wonder: two growing-stage questions tied to the course, one about a summer without bees and one a failure question about seeds that never came up.

## 2026-09-30 (pass HD): business for 9 to 12, the eighth strand begins

- Mikey committed HC, so the commit message starts fresh at HD. Business is a new subject, the fifteenth, ranked after Agriculture.
- The course: `business-9`, Open for business, grade 9. Four modules: business-basics (goods and services; producers, manufacturers, wholesalers, retailers and service businesses; sole proprietorships, partnerships, corporations and LLCs, and limited liability; traditional, centrally planned, market and mixed economies and private enterprise; imports and exports; the four functions of a business; Adam Smith's pin factory of 1776 and specialization), marketing-mix-9 (the marketing concept and the functions of marketing; markets, segments, mass marketing and target markets; the four Ps; supply and demand in prices; wholesalers and retailers; marketing services against goods), business-money (revenue, expenses and profit; the income statement and the balance sheet; fixed and variable costs and break-even; the business cycle; the three jobs of money; credit and interest; gross and net pay), ethics-and-careers (ethical, moral and legal choices; the roles of government and the courts; organized labor and collective bargaining; a career plan).
- Standards, read this turn from the text TEA publishes today: the course is now §127.241, Principles of Business, Marketing, and Finance (One Credit), Adopted 2015, in Chapter 127, Subchapter F, in effect since August 1, 2025; the old Chapter 130 numbering is retired. Forty-six of its student expectations are mapped. The national column carries four of the Voluntary National Content Standards in Economics, second edition (Council for Economic Education, 2010), Standards 6, 7, 12 and 14, quoted exactly from the Council's own publication.

Both are citation accuracy, not content, and both will be checked against the published texts before they change.

- Stories at the older length with six pictures each (S3354 to S3377): Two Mowers and a Sign, The Hoodies Nobody Bought, The Bakery's Two Statements, The Cheaper Screens; and the long story Frederick and the Taco Truck (CS541 to CS546, 401 words, eight paragraphs, six pictures, Frederick at twenty-four with a used food truck, Character: C5): an LLC, a lunch crowd that never came, fifteen hundred tacos to break even, a hospital at midnight, and a first year on two sheets of paper. Every figure in the stories was worked out and checked: the lawn money splits evenly, the hoodie orders net $205, the bakery's assets equal its liabilities plus equity, and the taco truck's months come out $600 short and $1,200 ahead. The flow count stays at 135, and the ledger holds 4,002 pictures marked Needed.
- The game: Break Even, a new kind (`breakeven`, the app's forty-ninth), owned by the course: a small business's fixed costs, price and cost per item, a chart of the revenue line and the total-cost line, and four counts to choose from; the right count marks the crossing. `BREAKEVEN_ITEMS`, `breakevenUnits` and `breakevenRounds(round)` in logic.mjs build six businesses a round whose answers are always whole numbers, which the rules test proves, and the click-through plays a round.

The 2024 text (read in full from the Texas Administrative Code) reorganizes the course and adds expectations such as Texas's top commodities, regions of production and parliamentary procedure. - National economics standards: the economics plans quoted the Council for Economic Education's standards as short paraphrases, and several only as titles, such as "Scarcity." ## 2026-09-30 (pass HF): business for 6 to 8

- Mikey has not committed HD or HE, so the commit message covers HD, HE and HF.
- The course: `business-6`, Earn, save, start, grade 6 under Business, for grades 6 to 8, built above the Texas minimum. Four modules: earning-and-taxes (gross and net pay; income tax, with no Texas state income tax but a federal one and Social Security and Medicare; sales tax at Texas's 6.25 percent state rate plus up to 2 percent local, 8.25 at most; what training does to lifetime pay; savings, grants, scholarships, loans and work-study), banking-and-credit (checking accounts and their fees; debit against credit; a check register; credit history, the credit report, seven years for most late payments, and the report's value to borrowers and lenders), budgets-and-net-worth (the parts of a budget and their percents; fixed and variable expenses; assets, liabilities and a net worth statement; simple against compound interest), starting-a-small-business (spotting a need, costs and profit, pricing against competitors, and sales, coupons and rebates compared with arithmetic).

The flow count stays at 135, and the ledger holds 4,032 pictures marked Needed.
- The game: The Better Deal, a new kind (`deal`, the app's fiftieth), owned by the course: a price tag and two coupons, a percent off and dollars off; tap the one that saves more, and both savings and final prices appear. `DEAL_ITEMS`, `dealSaving` and `dealRounds(round)` in logic.mjs build six items a round with whole-dollar savings and never a tie, which the rules test proves, and the click-through plays a round.

- Order games: a cycle, such as a frog's life, has no natural first step; egg and adult are both reasonable starts. A real fingerprint cannot be tested in the test browser, so Mikey should try it on his phone.

The old row drew every label on one line in a box sized for about twelve letters. `flowLayout` in logic.mjs now keeps the row only when every label is short; any longer label turns the chain into a column of wide boxes, top to bottom, with each label wrapped onto one or two lines and the type a little larger than before. The rules test runs every flow diagram in every lesson through it, and a measurement in the browser with the app's own fonts found all 821 labels in the 237 diagrams that switch to the column inside their boxes.
- A flaky check, fixed at its root: the click-through reloaded a lesson a quarter second after opening it and once beat the save, failing three lesson checks in a row; it now waits for the save itself before reloading.

## 2026-10-01 (pass HH): business for K to 2, and the depth program's strands are complete

## 2026-10-01 (pass HI): the Key idea card, and two questions answered

- Mikey has not committed HD to HH, so the commit message covers HD through HI.
- The Key idea card: in the dark theme it now uses the light green of the mastered lists and notes (#AAD8C5) with dark text (#16201B), and its title and text are centered in both themes; the light theme keeps its gold, as the frozen light theme requires. The click-through checks the color, the dark text and the centered title.
- Licensing, explained in `docs/LICENSING-AND-HOSTING.md`: the app checks codes with a public key, which cannot create codes, so shipping it is safe; the private key must never be in any repository, and the development key's private half in `tests/fixtures/` must be replaced by a real pair before launch. A code that runs only in the browser cannot stop sharing, editing the app, or reading the paid lessons, which today ship inside the one page. The stronger options, online activation and serving paid lessons only after activation, both need a small server and an honest change to the privacy note. Recommendation: launch with names and dates on codes, and build the server option when the curriculum is the asset to protect.
- Hosting, explained in the same doc: a private repository hides the source history and docs but not the built page every browser downloads. Cloudflare Pages and Vercel both deploy private repositories; Vercel's free Hobby plan is non-commercial only (Vercel's fair use guidelines), so a product that sells licenses needs Pro, while Cloudflare's free plan allows business use. Devices load the site the same way on either. The real cost is the move: saved data lives per web address, so every device needs a backup at the old address and a restore at the new one, once, to the permanent domain.
- Next: a college course (proposed Business Principles, anchored to BUSI 1301 in Texas's Academic Course Guide Manual, read before citing), then pictures.

Nutrition is taught without calorie targets or weight talk.

- The course: `sexual-health-6`, Growing up healthy, grade 6 under Health. Four modules: puberty-and-the-body-6 (changes during puberty and the wide range of timing, about 8 to 13 for girls and 9 to 14 for boys; hygiene; the pituitary gland and the hormones; the menstrual cycle, with periods of 2 to 7 days and early cycles of 21 to 45 days), reproduction-and-pregnancy-6 (fertilization in a fallopian tube and the zygote; about 40 weeks, a heartbeat on ultrasound by about week 6, major organs by week 12, a detailed scan around week 20; folic acid and prenatal care; goals before parenthood), healthy-relationships-6 (friendship, infatuation, dating and marriage; friendship as the foundation; what healthy looks like; anger, control, jealousy, manipulation and isolation as warning signs; boundaries, including about touch, and refusal skills), safety-and-choices-6 (forms of abuse and exploitation, never the victim's fault; telling and reporting; Texas's reporting law and the federal duty of schools to stop harassment; the risks of sexual activity, STIs defined as the standard defines them, and abstinence, the only 100% effective method, as Texas law requires).
- Standards: all 27 expectations of the grade 6 health standard's reproductive and sexual health strand, §115.26(b)(20) to (23), quoted exactly from the full text read in the Texas Administrative Code, and three National Health Education Standards. The plan row carries the course's id, since grade 6 Health now has two rows.
- Approach: clinical, plain, protective and age-appropriate. The stories are about growing up, family, friendship, boundaries and getting help, never romance: a boy and a growth chart, a niece and her aunt's twenty-week ultrasound, a friendship that turned controlling, and a student who reported harassment in a group chat. The long story, Savanah and the Smallest Heartbeat (CS583 to CS588, 408 words, Character: C8), follows Savanah helping with a fetal echocardiogram at twenty weeks: a heart rate of about 145, inside the normal 110 to 160, four chambers, a heartbeat since about week 6, and prenatal care. Short stories S3524 to S3547. The first run had two long sentences, three stories under length and one picture with no word in common with its paragraph; all fixed, and the picture audit is at zero.
- The game: Green Flag or Red Flag, a new kind (`greenred`, the app's fifty-sixth; `flag` was taken by a lesson picture type), owned by the course: one behavior at a time, sorted as a sign of a healthy relationship or a warning sign; `GREENRED_CARDS` and `greenredRounds(round)` deal four of each a round, which the rules test proves, and the click-through plays a round.
- Wonder: two teen-stage questions tied to the course, growing at different speeds, and a failure question about laughing along at a joke that crossed a line.
- Two safeguards added in the next turn, after the course itself was built. First, consent: Texas requires schools to obtain a parent's written consent before a student receives human sexuality instruction (Texas Education Code §28.004(i-2), added in 2021, read in the Legislature's own text). The course carries `consent: 'human-sexuality'`, and ticking it in any course list opens a box that says so and assigns it only when the educator confirms "A parent has consented"; Cancel leaves it unassigned. The click-through ticks it and cancels. Second, Wonder questions: a question was offered when it was tied to the course a student was working in or simply matched the student's age stage, so the two questions tied to this course could have reached any teen. The new rule, `wonderAllowed`, keeps questions tied to a consent course inside that course, and the rules test proves both directions. (The flag first landed on the high school health course, because this course's elective line carries a comment; the test caught it.)
- The browser test that reloads a lesson failed again, and its diagnostics showed why the earlier fixes missed: before the reload the where-note was right, and after it the app was on the welcome screen with no note, because the restore found no saved record for the student yet. Saves are single, whole writes, so nothing was half-saved; the reload simply came before this new student's very first save. The test now waits for the record itself, with its events, before reloading.
- Next: reproductive and sexual health for grades 9 to 12, on Health I's strand, citing only the expectations whose published wording was read complete; then the college courses.

## 2026-10-01 (pass HQ): the refresh test's real cause, found and fixed

- Mikey said "continue" without a commit word, so the last passes count as uncommitted and the commit message covers HM through HQ. Pass HP's files and preview were handed over at the start of this turn; the tool limit had cut the previous turn off just after packaging.
- The intermittent failure of "a refresh brings the student back to the lesson they were on" (four times since pass HG) is explained. A probe cut from the click-through, run six times with every stored key and event count printed around the reload, failed twice, and both times the store was empty after the reload: the student's record and the where-note were gone, though both were present a moment before and nothing in the app or the test page deletes them. The test's stand-in for durable storage is localStorage, and Chromium commits a page's localStorage writes in batches; the reload sometimes started before the newest batch was committed, so the new page opened with an empty store and the app correctly fell back to the welcome screen.
- The earlier explanations were wrong, and are corrected here: pass HM blamed a race with the welcome screen (reading the note first is still sound, but was not the cause), and pass HO blamed a record not yet saved (the record had been saved; the store itself came back empty).
- The fix is in the test, because real storage is durable: before the reload, the test copies the whole store and registers a one-time script that puts back any missing key when the page reloads, guarded by a token in window.name, which survives a reload. The probe was deleted.
- Open item: on a real phone, answer a question and refresh at once, to confirm a real browser never drops the newest answer the way headless Chromium can.
- Next: General Psychology at the college level (PSYC 2301), then the other college courses, one per pass.

## 2026-10-01 (pass HR): practice resumes after a refresh, with every answer kept

- Mikey committed through HQ, so the commit message starts fresh at HR.
- Mikey tested on a real phone: he answered a question in Draw the Shapes, tapped Next and refreshed, and the app returned him to the lesson without his answer. Two gaps caused it: the where-note covered only the overview, lesson, story and map screens, not practice; and a set's answers lived only in memory until the set was finished and recorded.
- Now the practice screen is noted too, and while a set is in progress the note carries the set itself: its questions, the number reached, the answers so far, any feedback showing, and for placement the walk through the grades. A refresh restores the set exactly where it was. Leaving the practice screen for anywhere else drops the saved set, so a finished set can never come back and be recorded twice.
- The browser test now follows Mikey's steps on a quick check: answer, tap Next, refresh (carrying the store, as in pass HQ), and expect the next question with one answer kept. A probe found that my first version noted a screen called question; the practice screen is called practice, and an older test step that waits for question had been timing out quietly. That wait is left as it was, but noted here.
- Mikey's second report, from three screenshots of the agriculture course in walkthrough: the same question appeared three times in one ten-question round, which gives the answer away. The cause was general: a round draws ten questions from a module's banks, and the builder retried a duplicate only sixteen times before keeping it, so any module whose banks hold fewer different questions than its round had to repeat. An audit (tools/question-pools.mjs, report in docs/QUESTION-POOLS.md) found 137 of 603 modules in that position, in every subject.
- Now no round repeats a question: when a bank has nothing new, the module's other banks are tried, and if none has a new question the round ends early. Mastery already scales with the questions actually asked (coreTotal), so a shorter round keeps the same bar. The memory checks and placement probes skip a slot instead of repeating. The two agriculture banks from the screenshots grew from 7 questions to 13, every answer taught in its lesson, which leaves 135 short modules.
- Moving forward: the rules test builds rounds for every module and fails on any repeat, and a ratchet test fails if more than 135 modules fall short, so no new course can add a short bank and the limit only moves down as banks grow. Every new module needs at least as many different questions as its round, and a few more so repeat rounds vary.
- Growing the 135 short banks is now a standing program, a batch of modules per pass, each new question's answer taught in its lesson.
- Next: grow the short question banks, a batch per pass (the report lists them), then General Psychology at the college level (PSYC 2301) and the other college courses.

## 2026-10-01 (pass HS): what counts as a repeat, and the first batch of banks grown

- Mikey has not committed HR, so the commit message covers HR and HS.
- Pass HR's no-repeat rule judged two questions the same when their words matched, which was wrong for picture tasks: "Tap the bigger one" with two different pairs of pictures is two questions. Under that rule a pre-K round of five such tasks stopped after two. One definition now decides what a repeat is, `questionKey` in logic.mjs: the setup, the wording, the picture, the answer and the set of answers offered, in any order. Rounds, memory checks, placement probes, the audit and the rules tests all use it, and the pre-K round is back to five.
- Tracing and dot-to-dot tasks (type trace) are motor practice with no answer to give away, so tracing a letter twice is allowed, and modules made only of tracing are left out of the audit.
- Four early modules teach a two-way contrast by design (red and blue, one and two dots, A and B, sun and moon), so four different questions is the honest maximum and their rounds run four instead of five. Growing them would mean new picture variety, such as different red and blue objects, which is a decision for Mikey.
- First batch grown, the seven core grade 6 modules: mean, median and mode and the four quadrants gained computed questions (new numbers and new points each round, hundreds of different questions); plot and conflict, poetry, microscopes and the supply chain gained fixed questions with every answer taught in the lesson; plate tectonics gained a small fixed bank. The audit now lists 88 short modules, down from 135, and the rules test's limit is 88.
- A mistake caught before delivery: my script inserted the plot and poetry questions into the grade 1 music beat bank, because the reading banks end their list with a trailing comma and the search matched the next bank in the file. The music bank was restored from the delivered copy and checked identical, and the reading questions were inserted inside their own banks. The insertion helper now works only inside the named bank's own block.
- Next: the next batch of short banks (grade 6 electives, then grade 9), then General Psychology (PSYC 2301).

## 2026-10-02 (pass IB): the question review begins, with everything the checks can see fixed across the app

- Mikey committed through IA and asked for a review of every question and answer, so each one makes sense when a person reads it, in his conversational voice. The commit message starts fresh at IB.
- The size of it: 1,344 banks make 9,352 question patterns once numbers are folded together. That is too many to read well in one pass, so the review is a program. This pass fixes everything a check can find, across the whole app; the passes after it read every question by hand, grade by grade from pre-K up, then the Wonder questions and the game cards. General Psychology follows the program.
- New tool: tools/question-review.mjs prints a grade's questions the way the practice screen shows them (question, setup line, answer, other choices, explanation), and its wording checks flag questions that end in a fragment, point at a setup they come before with that, leave a word hanging, say 1 meters or a 8, or explain with a label or by repeating the answer. They flagged 1,060 patterns.
- Fixed across the app, in about 100 banks: setup references now say this (Which stage is this?), and where the setup only said Think about it, the thing moved into the question (Is plastic a conductor or an insulator?, Which body system are the lungs part of?, What was an empresario?). Two-way flash cards became whole questions (Is this color warm or cool?, In what year did this happen?), and hanging endings became whole questions (What does famished mean?, What is one more than 7?); The model?, Purpose? and Then? are gone.
- Grade 1's true-or-false questions now ask In the story, did the dog run to the hill?, because the question comes before the story it asks about. Leader and Texas-fact explanations are sentences (The mayor leads a city., Austin is the capital of Texas., Six flags have flown over Texas.), and the body-system explanation agrees with its subject (the lungs belong, the heart belongs).
- Number words are tidied in one place for every bank (generateQuestion runs every question through tidyQuestion): 1 meter, 1 one, an 8-inch nose. A lone 1 only, so 0.1, 11 and 21 keep their plurals.
- 13 patterns remain flagged, all explanations that only repeat their answer (grade 7 weak evidence, grade 9 source credibility, grade 12 star stages); their grades' read-throughs fix them. A rules test holds the count at 13, and it may only fall.
- What the checks cannot see is why the read-through matters: a wrong fact, two defensible answers, a giveaway choice, a stiff sentence. The mascot question in pass IA had one of those, and no check would have caught it.
- Next: the read-through, pre-K 3 and pre-K 4 first.

Fragments became whole questions (Why does the straw look bent?, What happens to the other bulb?, Who made these rules law?, What did the New Deal change most?, Why is it so much milder?, Which is not one of the First Amendment's five freedoms?). Government counts ask whole questions (How many terms may a President serve?) and answer in sentences (A President may serve 2 terms). Branch explanations are sentences with the right verb (The courts decide what a law means). Are groceries a need or a want?

## 2026-10-02 (pass IK): Wonder questions, early and growing stages

- Mikey has not committed IH, II or IJ, so the commit message covers IH to IK.
- The early and growing Wonder questions were read in full: 136 of 264 (72 early, 64 growing), every prompt, all four voices and every closing question. The teen and grown stages (128) are next.
- Wonder text was written and approved by a person, so every change below is listed for Mikey's approval, and the code comment now says so.
- Newest research and accuracy: w-feelings-pass (scientist) said even a very strong feeling usually changes within minutes; a 2015 study that had people time their feelings (Verduyn and Lavrijsen) found fear often fades within an hour while sadness can last days, and it now says that. w-on-paper (scientist) said handwriting beats typing for memory; the famous 2014 study found that, but later, larger studies found the difference small or missing, and it now says both. w-same-river (prompt and scientist) said every cell gets replaced; many brain cells last a whole life, so it says most cells. w-same-water: water is hardly ever made or used up (plants split it and bodies make it), not never. w-slow-time: the brain has no single stopwatch for minutes, rather than no clock at all. w-growing-everyone-talked-at-once: attention follows one voice at a time, while ears take in all of them. w-early-lost-the-beat: removed a claim that feet find a beat faster than hands, which could not be sourced. w-three-governments (grandparent): the founders split power between the nation and the states, and the states share theirs with towns; three levels was not their design. w-say-sorry: saying sorry usually helps, not every time. w-smallest: billions and billions of bits in a grain of sand.
- Voice: the pre-K questions w-big-or-small and w-same-or-different spoke about children in the third person, as if to a parent; they now speak to the child, and so does w-fish-home.
- Even-handedness: w-need-or-want-really's grandparent said The oldest wisdom says; it now says One very old teaching says.
- Wording: every culture (not every people), airplanes, farther, tell a computer carefully, one doubled this week, a plant given a whole bucket (the artist no longer tells the child what they did), and many people (not nobody) dislike some pictures at first.
- Found, not changed: two Wonder questions ask Where does the sun go at night? (w-sun-night, with no course, and w-where-sun-goes, for kindergarten science). Keep both, merge them, or rewrite one: Mikey's call.

## 2026-10-02 (pass IL): Wonder questions, teen and grown, and the first new batch

- Mikey committed through IK, so the commit message starts again at IL.
- Mikey's decisions: merge the two sun questions; let the older Wonder questions draw on the authors he reads; and grow the pool well past one question per module, failure and hard feelings first, because each question can be seen only twice and a student who keeps failing a module is shown failure and feelings questions first.

Changes, listed for Mikey's approval: w-square-root-doubt no longer says people refused the proof for thousands of years (the Greeks found it, it upset them, and legend says they hid it). w-atoms-all-the-way: nearly every atom in you heavier than hydrogen was made in a star or in the blast when one died (not every atom heavier than hydrogen, since helium came from the Big Bang), and the grandparent's dust to dust is one old prayer, not the oldest prayers. w-teen-left-out gives the newest research: early studies tied being left out to physical-pain brain areas, and later work found the patterns differ more than first thought. w-teen-card-in-full: banks also earn fees from people who pay in full, so they earn the most, not the only money, from people who do not. w-teen-early-dollars: twenty dollars a month from age twelve does not beat a hundred a month from thirty at ordinary returns (it does beat forty), so the prompt says forty. w-grown-the-crowd-did-nothing adds newer research from street cameras, where someone usually does step in. w-grown-angry-unfair: the surge of anger eases, and the feeling can last hours when replayed (it no longer says it fades within minutes). Several research claims that could not be sourced were softened (w-teen-hard-week, w-same-size, w-teen-wrong-in-front, w-stuck-equation, w-two-real-wants). w-best-objection now names Karl Popper. Small wording: anyone can make something pretty; a birth most of the world does not hold sacred; copyright has already gone to court.
- New: twelve questions, six teen and six grown (failure 5, feelings 4, hard days 3), drawing on David Deutsch (problems are inevitable and soluble; progress comes from correcting errors), Karl Popper (conjectures and refutations; optimism is a duty), Socrates (wise because he knew he did not know), Naval Ravikant (desire as a deal to be unhappy until you get it; happiness as a skill), the Dalai Lama (compassion as a path to happiness; seeing a hard event from another angle) and Arthur Brooks (satisfaction as what you have over what you want; fluid and crystallized intelligence; success as a craving). Ideas are paraphrased, not quoted at length, and each science claim beside them is one the research supports (hedonic adaptation, giving to others, rumination, the two kinds of intelligence). Like every Wonder question, the new ones show only after an educator approves them in the app.
- Not used yet: The Tethered Soul and Hardworking Happiness, until Mikey confirms the titles and authors. Brett Hall's ideas largely restate Deutsch's, so he is not named.
- The Wonder pool program: 275 questions now. One per module would take 328 more (early 96, growing 71, teen 66, grown 95). Each pass from here adds a batch, failure and hard feelings first.
- Next: the next Wonder batch (early and growing, where the gap is widest) and the game cards.

## 2026-10-02 (pass IM): games, first look

- Mikey committed through IL, so the commit message starts again at IM. He confirmed the two books: The Untethered Soul by Michael Singer and Hardwiring Happiness by Rick Hanson.

- The game that opens first is play now. A new student gets the first game on the list, and funFirst moves the most playful kind on the list to the front (pong, catch, jump, path, maze, dots, jigsaw and the drag games before decks and quick fires). Every grade now opens on a playful game: dots in pre-K, catch, jump, Pong, balance or path from kindergarten to grade 12. Grade 11 and college had no playful game at all (their maps wait for paintings), so each gained one: Step on the powers of 2 (Algebra II) and Catch the primary sources (college history).
- Layout: tests/e2e/game-layout.mjs opens every game on five screens (a small phone, a phone, a phone held sideways, a tablet and a laptop) and looks for the page scrolling sideways, a board wider or taller than the screen, a piece outside its board, and words spilling out of a tile. Result: no problems on any screen. Boards you play in motion or by dragging are capped by the screen's height as well as its width, so they fit a sideways phone. Twenty-four tap-and-scroll games (lists of choices, steps and sentences) are taller than a sideways phone and scroll, which is fine because nothing has to be seen at once; the robot walk is one of them, and putting its grid beside its buttons on a sideways phone is an open item. The audit takes about fifteen minutes, so it runs on request, not in check.sh.
- Accuracy in the decks: Fraction twins uses grade 3 denominators, and no two cards are worth the same, so every true match counts. Grade 7 Texas history plays a new Texas deck (statehood, Sam Houston, San Jacinto, the Alamo, Spindletop, the Galveston storm, Juneteenth, Barbara Jordan, the Comanche and the Caddo) instead of world capitals, which moved to world geography. The college statistics course plays statistics words taught in its lessons instead of formulas. Science words corrected: an adaptation is a trait that helps a living thing survive, a plate is a piece of Earth's shell, natural selection means the fit survive and pass it on, igneous rock cooled from melted rock (not only lava), and metamorphic rock changed by heat and pressure. Grade 5 pairs a solution with one thing dissolved into another and a plain mixture with parts you can still see, since a solution is also a mixture.
- Not done yet: a hands-on play review of every game kind for fun, the spaceship game Mikey suggested, and the next Wonder batch.

## 2026-10-02 (pass IN): the space shooter, and games that match their course

- Mikey has not committed IM, so the commit message covers IM and IN. He is in no rush and asked that the long layout audit run during these game passes.
- The space shooter (Mikey's idea) is a new kind of game. Chips drift down; you tap where you want the ship, it glides there and fires straight up. Hit the chips that fit the rule. Hitting any other chip costs a point and says why (Rock is not one of the gases.), and a chip that reaches the ship's line simply passes. A keyboard works too: the arrow keys move the ship and the space bar fires. Twenty chips a round, scored like Catch.
- Three space shooters, each matched to its course: Even Numbers for grade 2 numbers, gases for grade 3 science (states of matter), and elements for grade 6 science (elements and compounds). The shooter ranks right after Pong in funFirst, so it is the first game open in grades 2, 3 and 6, and the two science courses no longer start on a deck or a quick fire.
- Games that did not match their course were replaced, since a game should teach what its course teaches. Grade 7 science sorted solids and liquids, a grade 3 idea, and now sorts producers and consumers from its energy-in-ecosystems lesson. Grade 12 science caught acids, a grade 10 chemistry idea, and now catches igneous rocks from its rock-cycle lesson. Grade 12 economics sorted US states and countries, and now sorts needs and wants from its budgets lesson. The two decks only those games used are gone; the acids deck still serves grade 10 chemistry.
- Every chip the new games use is named in its lesson's key idea first (even and odd numbers, gases, solids and liquids, elements and compounds, producers and consumers, igneous and sedimentary rocks, needs and wants), so nothing is played before it is taught.
- Other games whose content sits outside their course, for the next game pass: primes in grade 12 precalculus, elements and compounds in grade 9 biology, red and blue chips in grade 2 reading, circles in grade 1 civics. The robot walk on a sideways phone is still open, and so is the hands-on fun review of the single-use kinds.

## 2026-10-03 (pass IS): Wonder questions for younger learners, failure and hard feelings first

- Mikey committed through IR and opened a new chat (The Wise Human 3.0), so the commit message starts again at IS. The first attempt at this pass ran out of room after its research; this one wrote it.
- A miss from pass IK, owned and fixed: IK's entry says the unsourced claim that feet find a beat before hands was removed from w-early-lost-the-beat. It left the scientist's written voice but stayed in the line pre-readers hear aloud. That line now says Tap your foot first. Then your hands can follow., which matches the written advice and claims nothing.
- Pre-readers now hear the choices. The Wonder screen for children who cannot read spoke the question but never the choices, and a tap answers at once, so a child chose blind (a code comment said the choices were read aloud; they were not). Now the question is read, then each choice in turn with Or before the last, and the choice being read lights up; Say it again repeats all of it (wonderSpokenParts in logic.mjs, wonderLit in ui.jsx). A rules test holds every spoken early question to its choices.
- Twenty-four new questions, listed here for Mikey's approval; like every Wonder question, none reaches a student until an educator approves it in the app. Early questions are shared across the whole early stage, so each had to make sense to a three-year-old and an eight-year-old alike; their choices stay short enough to hold in mind when heard.
- Early, failure: You spilled water on your picture. Is it ruined? / You tried and tried to zip your coat, and it kept getting stuck. What now? / You colored outside the lines. Is that a mistake? / You kicked at the ball and missed it. What do you do next? / Your shoes are on the wrong feet. Is that a big mistake?
- Early, feelings: When you miss someone you love, what can you do? / Your friends are playing a game and did not ask you. How do you feel? / When a feeling gets very big, can slow breaths help it get smaller? / Is it okay to feel shy when you meet someone new? / A grown-up said no to something you really wanted. What helps the cross feeling?
- Early, hard days: A job feels long and boring, and you want to stop. What could help you keep going? / When you are very tired, do little problems feel bigger?
- Growing, failure: You were completely sure of an answer, and it turned out to be wrong. How did that feel, and what did you do next? / Your team lost, and your mistake was part of the reason. What do you say to your team? / You practiced a piece all week, and at the recital your fingers froze. Does that mean the practice was wasted? / Your writing came back covered in corrections. Is that a bad sign? / You tried out for a team or a play and did not make it. What would you do next?
- Growing, feelings: Sometimes a voice in your head says you are bad at something. Do you have to believe it? / Why can one mean comment stick in your mind longer than ten nice ones? / You found out about a party you were not invited to. What do you do with that feeling? / Your heart races before a test or a game. Is that feeling helping you or getting in your way? / When you make a mistake, what do you say to yourself? Would you say the same thing to a friend?
- Growing, hard days: When you are bored, should you grab a screen right away, or let your mind wander for a while? / On days after you slept badly, does everything seem to go wrong?
- The research behind the claims, read before use: the Batman effect (White, Prager, Schaefer, Kross, Duckworth and Carlson, Child Development 2017: 180 children aged four and six kept at a boring task longest when they pretended to be a hard-working character); a skipped nap (Berger and others, Journal of Sleep Research 2012: toddlers showed more upset and fewer smiles, a small study of ten children); less sleep at 8 to 12 (Vriend and others, Journal of Pediatric Psychology 2013: an hour less for four nights weakened emotion regulation, enjoyment, memory and attention); the hypercorrection effect (Butterfield and Metcalfe 2001, replicated with children); self-distancing in fifth graders (Kross and others, Emotion 2011); self-compassion at 10 to 19 (Marsh, Chan and MacBeth, Mindfulness 2018, nineteen studies; a link, not proof of cause, and the skeptic says so); being left out in the Cyberball game (Hartgerink and others, PLOS ONE 2015, 120 studies); the negativity bias and its early start (Vaish, Grossmann and Woodward, Psychological Bulletin 2008); long breaths out (Balban and others, Cell Reports Medicine 2023, adults); the spotlight effect (Gilovich, Medvec and Savitsky 2000); feedback (Hattie and Timperley 2007); the default mode network (Buckner, Andrews-Hanna and Schacter 2008).
- Mikey's authors, attributed and paraphrased: Michael Singer (you are not the voice in your head; you are the one who hears it), Rick Hanson (stay with a good moment for a few extra seconds and let it sink in), Arthur Brooks (boredom turns the mind toward big questions, framed as his argument).
- Told honestly where the evidence is thin: saying I am excited made people feel more excited in Alison Wood Brooks's 2014 study, but a 2025 direct replication found no change in how they performed, and a 2024 review of 44 results found a small average help, mostly in adults (Bosshard and Gomez, Scientific Reports), so the racing-heart question says the help was small and mostly studied in grown-ups. A few studies found more ideas after boredom, and the question says not every study agrees. Inner speech varies a lot between people, and whether anyone has none at all is disputed (Nedergaard and Lupyan 2024 and a 2025 reply), so the question says only that it varies.
- The pool: 299 questions (early 83, growing 76, teen 62, grown 78); failure and feelings now early 45 and growing 39. One per module would take 304 more (early 84, growing 59, teen 66, grown 95).
- Checks: rules 1,887 and spelling 77; check.sh steps 1 to 11 passed in one run (browser click-through 300), and the turn ended before steps 12 and 13, which then passed on their own (dark theme 55, standalone page 8).
- Next: Mikey's phone review list (pass IT), then the next Wonder batch.
